}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}a\in M
and
b\notin M
, and
M
is maximal in this sense.

Let
B=A/\sim
, where
a\sim b
iff
a\leftrightarrow b\in M
for
a,b\in A
. Since
M
is a
-filter, operations on
\mathfrak{A}
induce operations on
B
and turn it into a modal algebra
\mathfrak{B}
. Let
f:A\to B
be the projection map. It is straightforward to verify that
f
is a surjective modal algebra homomorphism. Moreover,
f(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}a)=1
and
f(b)\neq 1
since
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}a\in M
and
b\notin M
.

By the correspondence between
-filters of
\mathfrak{B}
and
-filters of
\mathfrak{A}
containing
M
, if
F^{\prime}
is a
-filter on
\mathfrak{B}
such that
\{1\}\subsetneq F^{\prime}
, then its corresponding
-filter on
\mathfrak{A}
contains
b
by the maximality of
M
, so
f(b)\in F^{\prime}
. It follows that
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}f(b)\in F^{\prime}
. So,
\uparrow\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}f(b)
is the smallest
-filter of
\mathfrak{B}
properly containing
\{1\}
. Therefore,
B
is s.i.
∎

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}
be a finite s.i.
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebra and
D\subseteq A
. Then, for any
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebra
\mathfrak{B}
,

\mathfrak{B}\not\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},D)\text{ iff there is a s.i.~homomorphic image $\mathcal{C}$ of $\mathfrak{B}$ such that $\mathfrak{A}\hookrightarrow_{D}\mathcal{C}$}.

Proof.

Suppose that there is a s.i. homomorphic image
\mathcal{C}
of
\mathfrak{B}
and a stable embedding
h:\mathfrak{A}\hookrightarrow_{D}\mathcal{C}
. Define a valuation
V_{A}
on
\mathfrak{A}
by
V_{A}(p_{a})=a
. It follows from the definition of
\Gamma
and
\Delta
(
Definition

2.5
) that
V_{A}(\gamma)=1
for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
and
V_{A}(\delta)\neq 1
for all
\delta\in\Delta
. Thus,
V_{A}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}\bigwedge\Gamma)=1
and
V_{A}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}\delta)=\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}V_{A}(\delta)\neq 1
for all
\delta\in\Delta
. Since
\mathfrak{A}
is s.i.,
\mathfrak{A}
has an opremum
c
by
Proposition

2.1
. So, for each
\delta\in\Delta
, there is
n\in\omega
such that
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

. Hence,
\bigvee\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

, which implies
\mathfrak{A}\not\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},D)
. Next, define a valuation
V_{C}
on
\mathcal{C}
by

. The same argument as in the proof of

3.9
shows that

for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
and

for all
\delta\in\Delta
. Thus,
V_{C}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

and
V_{C}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}V_{C}(\delta)\neq 1
for all
\delta\in\Delta
. Since
\mathcal{C}
is a homomorphic image of
\mathfrak{B}
and
\mathfrak{B}\models\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
,
\mathcal{C}\models\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
as well. Since
\mathcal{C}
is also s.i., applying the same argument as for
\mathfrak{A}
, we obtain that
V_{C}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}\bigwedge\Gamma)=1

\bigvee\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}V_{C}(\delta)
is below or equal to the opremum of
\mathcal{C}
, so
\mathcal{C}\not\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},D)
. It follows that
\mathfrak{B}\not\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},D)
since
\mathcal{C}
is a homomorphic image of
\mathfrak{B}
.

Conversely, suppose that
\mathfrak{B}\not\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},D)
. Then there is a valuation
V_{B}
on
\mathfrak{B}
such that
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}V_{B}(\bigwedge\Gamma)\not\leq V_{B}(\bigvee\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}\delta)
. By
Lemma

5.4
, there is a s.i. homomorphic image
\mathcal{C}
of
\mathfrak{B}
and a valuation
V_{C}
on
\mathcal{C}
such that
V_{C}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}\bigwedge\Gamma)=1
and
V_{C}(\bigvee\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\leq m}\delta)\neq 1
. Define a map
h:\mathfrak{A}\to\mathcal{C}
by
h(a)=V_{C}(p_{a})
. Unfolding the definition of
\Gamma
and
\Delta
(
Definition

2.5
), it is straightforward to verify that
h
is a stable embedding satisfying CDC for
D
.
∎

Combining
Lemma

5.2
and
Theorem

5.5
, we obtain the following theorem, which is a version of
Theorem

3.9
for stable canonical formulas for pre-transitive logics.

Theorem 5.6
.

For any formula
\varphi
, there exist stable canonical formulas
\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{1},D_{1}),\dots
,
\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{n},D_{n})
where each
\mathfrak{A}_{i}
is a finite s.i.
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebra and
D_{i}\subseteq A_{i}
, such that for any s.i. modal algebra
\mathfrak{B}
,

\mathfrak{B}\models\varphi\text{ iff }\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge\{\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{i},D_{i}):1\leq i\leq n\}.

Proof.

This follows directly from
Lemma

5.2
and
Theorem

5.5
.
∎

Now we arrive at the axiomatization result for logics above
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
, generalizing the result for logics above
\mathsf{K4}
(
[
7
, Theorem 6.10]
).

Theorem 5.7
.

Let
m\geq 1
. Every logic
L\supseteq\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
is axiomatizable over
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
by stable canonical formulas. Moreover, if
L
is finitely axiomatizable over
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
, then
L
is axiomatizable over
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
by finitely many stable canonical formulas.

Proof.

Let
L
be a logic containing
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
. Then
L=\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}+\{\varphi_{i}:i\in I\}
for a set
\{\varphi_{i}:i\in I\}
of formulas. By
Theorem

5.6
, for each formula
\varphi_{i}
, there exists a finite set of stable canonical formulas
\{\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{ij},D_{ij}):1\leq j\leq n_{i}\}
such that for any s.i.
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebras
\mathfrak{B}
,
\mathfrak{B}\models\varphi
iff
\mathfrak{B}\models\{\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{ij},D_{ij}):1\leq j\leq n_{i}\}
. So, for any s.i.
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebra
\mathfrak{B}
,
\mathfrak{B}\models L
iff
\mathfrak{B}\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{ij},D_{ij})
for all
i\in I
and
1\leq j\leq n_{i}
. Since every logic is determined by the class of its s.i. modal algebras, it follows that
L=\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}+\{\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{ij},D_{ij}):i\in I,1\leq j\leq n_{i}\}
. Moreover, if
L
is finitely axiomatizable over
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
, then we can choose
I
to be finite, hence the set
\{\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{ij},D_{ij}):i\in I,1\leq j\leq n_{i}\}
is also finite.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

We have a computability result similar to
Remark

3.11
. Since the logic
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
is finitely axiomatizable and has the fmp (
Corollary

4.4
), it is decidable. Thus, the enumeration in the proof of
Lemma

5.2
is computable. Therefore, the result of
Theorem

5.7
is computable; that is, there is an algorithm that, given a finite axiomatization of
L
over
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
, computes a finite set of stable canonical formulas that axiomatize
L
over
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
.

Finally, we briefly discuss two extreme types of stable canonical formulas, namely, stable canonical formulas
\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},D)
with
D=\emptyset
and those with
D=A
.

Definition 5.9
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}
be a finite modal algebra.

1.

A stable canonical formula
\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},\emptyset)
is called a
stable formula
.

2.

A stable canonical formula
\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},A)
is called a
Jankov formula
.

It follows immediately from
Theorem

5.5
that for any
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebra
\mathfrak{B}
,

\mathfrak{B}\not\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},\emptyset)\text{ iff $\mathfrak{A}$ is a stable subalgebra of a s.i.~homomorphic image of $\mathfrak{B}$}

and

\mathfrak{B}\not\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},A)\text{ iff $\mathfrak{A}$ is a subalgebra of a s.i.~homomorphic image of $\mathfrak{B}$.}

The terminology Jankov formulas is motivated by the fact that this semantic characterization coincides with the original
Jankov formula

[
21
]
(see also
[
23
]
) for Heyting algebras.

It turns out that stable formulas and Jankov formulas axiomatize logics with special features.

Definition 5.10
.

Let
\mathsf{M}
be a logic.

1.

A class
\mathcal{K}
of
\mathsf{M}
-agebras is
\mathsf{M}
-stable
if for any
\mathsf{M}
-algebra
\mathfrak{A}
and any
\mathfrak{B}\in\mathcal{K}
, if
\mathfrak{A}\hookrightarrow_{\emptyset}\mathfrak{B}
, then
\mathfrak{A}\in\mathcal{K}
.

2.

A logic
L\supseteq\mathsf{M}
is
\mathsf{M}
-stable
if
\mathcal{V}(L)
is generated by an
\mathsf{M}
-stable class.

The notion of
\mathsf{M}
-stable logics is introduced and studied in
[
8
]
(see also
[
20
]
for a comprehensive account). It was shown that each
\mathsf{K4}
-stable logic is axiomatized by stable formulas over
\mathsf{K4}
. This result readily generalizes to pre-transitive logics
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
. We only sketch the proof as it is very similar to the one for
\mathsf{K4}
.

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
m\geq 1
. Every
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-stable logic is axiomatizable by stable formulas over
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
.

Proof Sketch.

Since
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
admits definable filtration, following the proof of
[
8
, Theorem 3.8]
, we can show that
\mathcal{V}(L)
is generated by a
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-stable class
\mathcal{K}
of finite
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebras. Also,
[
8
, Lemma 4.5]
holds for
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
because for any finite
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-space
\mathcal{F}
, adding an extra root to
\mathcal{F}
so that it sees every point in
\mathcal{F}
and itself results in another
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-space. Thus, following the proof of
[
8
, Theorem 4.7]
and using
Lemma

5.1
, we obtain the statement.
∎

Note that the converse does not hold. A logic axiomatized by a stable formula over
\mathsf{K4}
that is not
\mathsf{K4}
-stable can be found in
[
8
, Example 4.11]
.

Moreover, the fmp result regarding
\mathsf{M}
-stable logics
[
8
, Proposition 3.3]
generalizes as follows.

Theorem 5.12
.

Let
\mathsf{M}
be a logic that admits definable filtration. Then, every
\mathsf{M}
-stable logic has the fmp.

Proof.

Let
L
be an
\mathsf{M}
-stable logic. For any
\varphi\notin L
, there is an
L
-algebra
\mathfrak{A}
such that
\mathfrak{A}\not\models\varphi
, witnessed by some valuation
V
on
\mathfrak{A}
. Note that
\mathfrak{A}
is also an
\mathsf{M}
-algebra since
\mathsf{M}\subseteq L
. By the assumption that
\mathcal{M}
admits definable filtration, there is a finite subformula-closed set
\Theta^{\prime}
containing
\mathsf{Sub}(\varphi)
and a definable filtration
(\mathfrak{A}^{\prime},V^{\prime})
of
(\mathfrak{A},V)
for
\mathsf{Sub}(\varphi)
through
\Theta^{\prime}
such that
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}(\mathsf{M})
. Since
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow_{\emptyset}\mathfrak{A}
by the definition of definable filtrations and
\mathfrak{A}\in\mathcal{V}(L)
, we have
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}(L)
by
L
being
\mathsf{M}
-stable. Thus,
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
is a finite
L
-algebra that refutes
\varphi
. Hence,
L
has the fmp.
∎

Corollary 5.13
.

For
m\geq 1
, every
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-stable logic has the fmp.

Proof.

This follows from
Theorem

4.3
and
Theorem

5.12
.
∎

Remark 5.14
.

By a similar argument, one can also generalize the characterization results in
[
8
]
(e.g., Theorem 3.8 and Theorem 4.7), replacing filtration with definable filtration and
\mathsf{K4}
with
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
. These characterizations and
Proposition

5.11
are used in
[
30
, Chapter 4]
to obtain a general fmp result, including the fmp of all
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-stable logics as stated in
Corollary

5.13
.

Next, we turn to Jankov formulas, which axiomatize exactly union-splittings.

Definition 5.15
.

Let
X
be a complete lattice. A
splitting pair
of
X
is a pair
(x,y)
of elements of
X
such that
x\not\leq y
and for any
z\in X
, either
x\leq z
or
z\leq y
. If
(x,y)
is a splitting pair of
X
, we say that
x

splits

X
and
y
is a
splitting
in
X
.

This notion has been an important tool in the study of lattices of logics. See
[
16
, Section 10.7]
for a historical overview.

Definition 5.16
.

Let
L_{0}
and
L
be logics.

1.

L
is a
splitting
in
\mathsf{NExt}{L_{0}}
iff it is a lattice-theoretic splitting in the lattice
\mathsf{NExt}{L_{0}}
.

2.

L
is a
union-splitting
in
\mathsf{NExt}{L_{0}}
iff it is the join of a set of splittings in
\mathsf{NExt}{L_{0}}
.

Theorem 5.17
.

Let
m\geq 1
.

1.

A logic
L\in\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
is a splitting logic in
\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
iff
L=\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}+\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},A)
for some finite s.i.
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebra
\mathfrak{A}
.

2.

A logic
L\in\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
is a union-splitting logic in
\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
iff
L=\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}+\{\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{i},A_{i}):i\in I\}
for a set
\{\mathfrak{A}_{i}:i\in I\}
finite s.i.
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebras.

Proof.

(2) is an immediate consequence of (1). To show (1), first suppose that
L=\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}+\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},A)
for some finite s.i.
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebra
\mathfrak{A}
. We show that
(L,\mathsf{Log}(\mathfrak{A}))
is a splitting pair in
\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
. For any logic
L^{\prime}\in\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
such that
L\not\subseteq L^{\prime}
, there is a modal algebra
\mathfrak{B}
such that
\mathfrak{B}\models L^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{B}\not\models L
. Thus,
\mathfrak{B}\not\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},A)
, namely,
\mathfrak{A}
is a subalgebra of a homomorphic image of
\mathfrak{B}
. So,
L^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{Log}(\mathfrak{B})\subseteq\mathsf{Log}(\mathfrak{A})
. Hence,
(L,\mathsf{Log}(\mathfrak{A}))
is a splitting pair in
\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
.

Conversely, suppose that
L
is a splitting logic in
\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
. Then by
Theorem

5.18
below, since
\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
has the fmp (
Corollary

4.4
),
(L,\mathsf{Log}(\mathfrak{A}))
is a splitting pair in
\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
for some finite s.i.
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebra
\mathfrak{A}
. By the argument above,
(\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}+\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},A),\mathsf{Log}(\mathfrak{A}))
is a splitting pair in
\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
. It follows that
L=\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}+\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},A)
.
∎

A similar characterization of (union-)splitting logics in
\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{wK4}}
(where
\mathsf{wK4}=\mathsf{K}+\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

6
The
m
-closed domain condition and
m
-stable canonical formulas

In this section, we consider a variant of the closed domain condition and stable canonical formulas for pre-transitive logics. The intuitive idea is to preserve the modality not only one step but also up to
m
steps. This leads to an alternative axiomatization result to
Theorem

5.7
for logics in
\mathsf{NExt}{\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}}
.

holds. We also provide the frame-theoretic version of the definition and show that the two definitions are dual to each other.

Definition 6.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}=(X,R)
and
\mathfrak{Y}=(Y,Q)
be modal spaces and
f:X\to Y
be a stable map. For a clopen subset
D\subseteq Y
, we say that
f
satisfies the
m
-closed domain condition (
m
-CDC) for
D
if for all
1\leq k\leq m
,

Q^{k}[f(x)]\cap D\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow f(R^{k}[x])\cap D\neq\emptyset.

For a set
\mathcal{D}
of clopen subsets of
Y
, we say that
f:X\to Y
satisfies the
m
-closed domain condition (
m
-CDC) for
\mathcal{D}
if
f
satisfies
m
-CDC for all
D\in\mathcal{D}
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}
and
\mathfrak{B}
be modal algebras and
h:A\to B
be a stable homomorphism. For any
a\in A
,

h
satisfies
m
-CDC for
a
iff
h_{*}
satisfies
m
-CDC for
\beta(a)
.

For any
D\subseteq A
,

h
satisfies
m
-CDC for
D
iff
h_{*}
satisfies
m
-CDC for
\beta[D]
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{A}
and
\mathfrak{B}
be modal algebras with dual spaces
\mathfrak{X}=(X,R)
and
\mathfrak{Y}=(Y,Q)
and
h:A\to B
be a stable homomorphism. Let
a\in A
. It follows from the duality that

h
satisfies
m
-CDC for
a

\displaystyle\text{ iff }h(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{k}a)\leq\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{k}h(a)\text{ for all $1\leq k\leq m$}

\displaystyle\text{ iff }h_{*}^{-1}\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{k}\beta(a)\subseteq\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{k}h_{*}^{-1}\beta(a)\text{ for all $1\leq k\leq m$}

\displaystyle\text{ iff }Q^{k}[h_{*}(x)]\cap\beta(a)\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow h_{*}(R^{k}[x])\cap\beta(a)\neq\emptyset\text{ for $1\leq k\leq m$}

iff
h_{*}
satisfies
m
-CDC for
\beta(a)
.

The second statement follows directly from the first.
∎

Notation 6.4
.

We write
h:\mathfrak{A}\hookrightarrow^{m}_{D}\mathfrak{B}
if
h
is a stable embedding satisfying
m
-CDC for
D
and
\mathfrak{A}\hookrightarrow^{m}_{D}\mathfrak{B}
if there is such an
h
. We write
f:\mathfrak{X}\twoheadrightarrow^{m}_{\mathcal{D}}\mathfrak{Y}
if
f
is a surjective stable map satisfying
m
-CDC for
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathfrak{X}\twoheadrightarrow^{m}_{\mathcal{D}}\mathfrak{Y}
if there is such an
f
.

Now we define
m
-stable canonical formulas
for pre-transitive logics
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
(
m\geq 1
). The only difference with standard stable canonical formulas is that in
\Gamma
, the part using the closed domain
D
is generalized so that it captures
m
-CDC.

.

\mathfrak{A}

Remark 6.6
.

As stable canonical rules,
m
-stable canonical formulas can also be defined directly from finite modal spaces (i.e., finite Kripke frames). The basic idea is the same as
Definition

6.5
, but we use
\Gamma
and
\Delta
in
Definition

2.6
instead and change the last clause in
\Gamma
to

\{p_{x}\to\bigvee\{\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{k}p_{y}:y\in D\}:x\in X,D\in\mathcal{D},x\in(R^{-1})^{k}[D],1\leq k\leq m\}

in light of
Definition

6.2
.

Remark 6.7
.

In general,
m
-CDC is stronger than the standard CDC. For
m=1
, where the base logic is
\mathsf{K4}
, the
m
-CDC and
m
-stable canonical formulas reduce to the standard CDC and stable canonical formulas. It is clear that
m
-stable canonical formulas induce the same stable formulas (up to equivalence) as stable canonical formulas. Moreover, since a modal homomorphism always satisfies
m
-CDC for any
D
, they also induce the same Jankov formulas (up to equivalence).

Following the proof of
Theorem

5.5
, it is straightforward to verify the semantic condition of the validity of
m
-stable canonical formulas.

Theorem 6.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}
be a finite s.i.
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebra and
D\subseteq A
. Then, for any
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebra
\mathfrak{B}
,

\mathfrak{B}\not\models\gamma_{+}^{m}(\mathfrak{A},D)\text{ iff there is a s.i.~homomorphic image $\mathcal{C}$ of $\mathfrak{B}$ such that $\mathfrak{A}\hookrightarrow^{m}_{D}\mathcal{C}$}.

Now we show that any logic above
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
can be axiomatized by
m
-stable canonical formulas, thus providing an alternative to
Theorem

5.7
. Recall that in the case of stable canonical formulas, the essential idea was to construct finite refutation patterns as done in
Lemma

5.2
. The construction in turn essentially depends on the definable filtration developed in
Lemma

4.1
. Thus, to adapt the whole proof for stable canonical formulas to
m
-stable canonical formulas, it suffices to observe that the definable filtration in
Lemma

4.1
in fact induces a stable homomorphism satisfying
m
-CDC.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}=(A,\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

Hence, we conclude that
i^{\prime}
satisfies
m

.
∎

Theorem 6.10
.

For any formula
\varphi
, there exist stable canonical formulas
\gamma_{+}^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{1},D_{1}),\dots
,
\gamma_{+}^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{n},D_{n})
where each
\mathfrak{A}_{i}
is a finite s.i.
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
-algebra and
D_{i}\subseteq A_{i}
, such that for any s.i. modal algebra
\mathfrak{B}
,

\mathfrak{B}\models\varphi\text{ iff }\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge\{\gamma_{+}^{m}(\mathfrak{A}_{i},D_{i}):1\leq i\leq n\}.

Proof.

Lemma

6.9
shows that the condition (3) in
Lemma

5.2
can be strengthened such that the stable embedding
h:\mathfrak{A}_{i}\hookrightarrow_{D_{i}}\mathcal{C}
satisfies
m
-CDC for
D_{i}
. Thus, combined with
Theorem

6.8
, the statement follows.
∎

Corollary 6.11
.

Let
m\geq 1
. Any logic
L\supseteq\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
is axiomatizable over
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
by
m
-stable canonical formulas. Moreover, if
L
is finitely axiomatizable over
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
, then
L
is axiomatizable over
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
by finitely many
m
-stable canonical formulas.

Proof.

This follows from
Theorem

6.10
by a similar argument as in the proof of
Theorem

5.7
.
∎

Remark 6.12
.

The above results can be seen as an improvement of
Theorem

5.6
in the sense that every
m
-stable canonical formula is equivalent over
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
to a stable canonical formula, but not vice versa. It is easy to see from the semantic characterizations Theorems
5.5
and
6.8
that, any
m
-stable canonical formula
\gamma_{+}^{m}(\mathfrak{A},D)
is equivalent to the stable canonical formula
\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{A},D^{\prime})
, where
D^{\prime}=\{\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{k-1}d:d\in D,1\leq k\leq m\}
. The following example shows that the converse does not hold.

We construct a counterexample using finite modal spaces. Let
\mathfrak{X}
be the finite rooted
\mathsf{K4^{4}_{1}}
-space depicted as below and
D=\{d\}
.

\bullet\>a
\bullet\>b
\bullet\>c
\bullet\>d
\mathfrak{X}

Assume for a contradiction that the stable canonical formula
\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{X},\{D\})
is equivalent to some
m
-stable canonical formula
\gamma_{+}^{m}(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
. Then
\mathfrak{X}\not\models\gamma_{+}^{m}(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
and
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}\not\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{X},\{D\})
. By the dual of
Theorem

5.5
and
Theorem

6.8
, one can verify that
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{X}
. Let
\mathfrak{Y}
and
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}
be rooted
\mathsf{K4^{4}_{1}}
-spaces depicted as below.

\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\mathfrak{Y}
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}

Note that there is only one stable map from
\mathfrak{Y}
or
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}
to
\mathfrak{X}
. It is easy to see that
\mathfrak{Y}\twoheadrightarrow_{D}\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}\not\twoheadrightarrow_{D}\mathfrak{X}
, thus
\mathfrak{Y}\not\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{X},\{D\})
and
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}\models\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{X},\{D\})
.
If
d\in D^{\prime}
for some
D^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, then
\mathfrak{Y}\not\twoheadrightarrow^{m}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\mathfrak{X}
, so
\mathfrak{Y}\models\gamma_{+}^{m}(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
. If there is no
D^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
such that
d\in D^{\prime}
, then
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow^{m}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\mathfrak{X}
, so
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}\not\models\gamma_{+}^{m}(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
. This contradicts that
\gamma^{m}(\mathfrak{X},\{D\})
and
\gamma_{+}^{m}(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
are equivalent.

7
Future work

As we have discussed in this paper, admitting definable filtration and being able to define the master modality are the key points to generalize the theory of stable canonical formulas. Given the success with the logics
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
, one might expect a similar result for the logic
\mathsf{wK4}=\mathsf{K}+\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptp
and other pre-transitive logics
\mathsf{K4^{m}_{n}}
. However, even though
\mathsf{wK4}
has the fmp
[
9
]
, it is unknown whether it admits definable filtration (see, e.g.,
[
26
, Section 5]
). The fmp of pre-transitive logics in general, such as
\mathsf{K4}^{3}_{2}
, is a long-standing open problem (e.g.,
[
16
, Problem 11.2]
), let alone definable filtration. Stable canonical rules work well for
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{K}}
because rules have the master modality built into their semantics. Since the master modality is not definable in
\mathsf{K}
, it seems quite challenging to define stable canonical formulas for
\mathsf{K}
in a meaningful way. We leave it open to generalize stable canonical formulas to non-transitive logics other than
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
.

A noticeable application of stable canonical rules and canonical rules is to study
admissible
rules. Jeřábek
[
22
]
used canonical rules, and Bezhanishvili et al.
[
12
]
(see also
[
30
, Chapter 6]
) used stable canonical rules to give an alternative proof of the decidability of admissibility for transitive modal logics such as
\mathsf{K4}
and
\mathsf{S4}
, and the intuitionistic logic
\mathsf{IPC}
. We leave it open whether stable canonical rules or
m
-stable canonical rules can be used to study the admissibility in the pre-transitive logics
\mathsf{K4^{m+1}_{1}}
.
